# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Simple Examples
  Topic: Plugin authoring conventions
  Summary: Describes the convention of using factory functions for Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugins.
  Keywords: factory function, plugin, Vite, Rolldown, Rollup, options, customization
  Entities: factory function, plugin object, options

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Introduction to Rolldown hooks in Vite dev server
  Summary: Overview of how Vite dev server invokes Rolldown Build Hooks and the per-environment nature of these hooks.
  Keywords: dev, Vite dev server, plugin container, Rolldown Build Hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: Vite dev server, plugin container, Rolldown Build Hooks, per-environment hooks

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Rolldown Hooks → Hooks called on module request
  Topic: Hooks executed for each incoming module request
  Summary: List of Rolldown hooks called on each incoming module request: resolveId, load, and transform, along with extended options and importer behavior.
  Keywords: module request, resolveId, load, transform, extended options, importer value
  Entities: resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, SSR documentation, importer value, import analysis phase

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API
  Topic: Introduction to Vite Plugin API
  Summary: Overview of Vite plugins extending Rolldown's interface, recommendation to read Rolldown docs first, and advice on checking existing patterns before authoring.
  Keywords: Vite plugins, Rolldown, plugin interface, dev and build
  Entities: Rolldown, Vite

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add and configure plugins using the plugins array, handling falsy plugins, and using presets for complex features.
  Keywords: plugins array, vite.config.js, configuration, presets
  Entities: vite.config.js, plugins, defineConfig

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation patterns
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, illustrating the factory function pattern.
  Keywords: examples, transform, virtual modules, factory function
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

[2026-09-09T19:22:38.5282480Z] [OUTPUT:STAGE3_OUTPUT_segsegment-7] Length: 8595 chars (truncated to 40k for log)
{
  "chunks": [
    {
      "id": "plugin-api-simple-examples-001",

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

````markdown
## Vite Specific Hooks

Vite plugins can also provide hooks that serve Vite-specific purposes. These hooks are ignored by Rollup.

### `config`

- **Type:** `(config: UserConfig, env: { mode: 'build' | 'serve', command: string, isSsrBuild?: boolean, isPreview?: boolean }) => UserConfig | null | void`
- **Kind:** `async`, `sequential`
- **Scope:** [Global](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks)

  Modify Vite config before it's resolved. The hook recei
````

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add and configure plugins using the plugins array, handling falsy plugins, and using presets for complex features.
  Keywords: plugins array, vite.config.js, configuration, presets
  Entities: vite.config.js, plugins, defineConfig

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation patterns
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, illustrating the factory function pattern.
  Keywords: examples, transform, virtual modules, factory function
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

[2026-09-09T19:23:28.6019558Z] [INPUT:STAGE3_INPUT_segsegment-8_chunk6] Length: 2498 chars (truncated to 40k for log)

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

# SECTION METADATA

  Topic: Hooks specific to Vite functionality
  Summary: Introduction to Vite-specific hooks that are ignored by Rollup, starting with the config hook.

````markdown
## Vite Specific Hooks

Vite plugins can also provide hooks that serve Vite-specific purposes. These hooks are ignored by Rollup.

### `config`

- **Type:** `(config: UserConfig, env: { mode: 'build' | 'serve', command: string, isSsrBuild?: boolean, isPreview?: boolean }) => UserConfig | null | void`
- **Kind:** `async`, `sequential`
- **Scope:** [Global](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks)

  Modify Vite config before it's resolved. The hook recei
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API
  Topic: Introduction to Vite Plugin API
  Summary: Overview of Vite plugins extending Rolldown's interface, recommendation to read Rolldown docs first, and advice on checking existing patterns before authoring.
  Keywords: Vite plugins, Rolldown, plugin interface, dev and build
  Entities: Rolldown, Vite

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add plugins to devDependencies, configure them using the plugins array, and how to handle presets and falsy plugins.
  Keywords: plugins array, devDependencies, presets, falsy plugins
  Entities: vite.config.js, defineConfig, plugins

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation examples
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, including the convention of using factory functions.
  Keywords: Transforming, Virtual File, factory function, virtual modules
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown Hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

````markdown
- **Scope:** [Global](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks)
````

# SECTION METADATA

````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add plugins to devDependencies, configure them using the plugins array, and how to handle presets and falsy plugins.
  Keywords: plugins array, devDependencies, presets, falsy plugins
  Entities: vite.config.js, defineConfig, plugins

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation examples
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, including the convention of using factory functions.
  Keywords: Transforming, Virtual File, factory function, virtual modules
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown Hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

````markdown
## Vite Specific Hooks

- **Kind:** `async`, `sequential`
- **Scope:** [Global](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks)

  Modify Vite config before it's resolved. The hook recei
````

# SOURCE CONTENT

````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add plugins to devDependencies, configure them using the plugins array, and how to handle presets and falsy plugins.
  Keywords: plugins array, devDependencies, presets, falsy plugins
  Entities: vite.config.js, defineConfig, plugins

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation examples
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, including the convention of using factory functions.
  Keywords: Transforming, Virtual File, factory function, virtual modules
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown Hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add plugins to devDependencies, configure them using the plugins array, and how to handle presets and falsy plugins.
  Keywords: plugins array, devDependencies, presets, falsy plugins
  Entities: vite.config.js, defineConfig, plugins

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation examples
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, including the convention of using factory functions.
  Keywords: Transforming, Virtual File, factory function, virtual modules
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown Hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

[2026-09-09T19:27:03.5912092Z] [OUTPUT:STAGE3_OUTPUT_segsegment-12] Length: 6580 chars (truncated to 40k for log)

````markdown
## Vite Specific Hooks

Vite plugins can also provide hooks that serve Vite-specific purposes. These hooks are ignored by Rollup.

### `config`

- **Type:** `(config: UserConfig, env: { mode: 'build' | 'serve', command: string, isSsrBuild?: boolean, isPreview?: boolean }) => UserConfig | null | void`
- **Kind:** `async`, `sequential`
- **Scope:** [Global](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks)

  Modify Vite config before it's resolved. The hook recei
````

````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Plugins Config
  Topic: Configuring plugins in vite.config.js
  Summary: Explanation of how to add plugins to devDependencies, configure them using the plugins array, and how to handle presets and falsy plugins.
  Keywords: plugins array, devDependencies, presets, falsy plugins
  Entities: vite.config.js, defineConfig, plugins

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Plugins Config

Users will add plugins to the project `devDependencies` and configure them using the `plugins` array option.

```js [vite.config.js]
import vitePlugin from 'vite-plugin-feature'
import rollupPlugin from 'rollup-plugin-feature'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [vitePlugin(), rollupPlugin()],
})
```

Falsy plugins will be ignored, which can be used to easily activate or deactivate plugins.

`plugins` also accepts presets including several plugins as a single element. This is useful for complex features (like framework integration) that are implemented using several plugins. The array will be flattened internally.

```js
// framework-plugin
import frameworkRefresh from 'vite-plugin-framework-refresh'
import frameworkDevtools from 'vite-plugin-framework-devtools'

export default function framework(config) {
  return [frameworkRefresh(config), frameworkDevTools(config)]
}
```

```js [vite.config.js]
import { defineConfig } from 'vite'
import framework from 'vite-plugin-framework'

export default defineConfig({
  plugins: [framework()],
})
```
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Simple Examples
  Topic: Basic plugin implementation examples
  Summary: Examples of transforming custom file types and importing virtual files, including the convention of using factory functions.
  Keywords: Transforming, Virtual File, factory function, virtual modules
  Entities: fileRegex, myPlugin, virtualModuleId, resolvedVirtualModuleId, exactRegex, @rolldown/pluginutils

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Simple Examples

:::tip
It is common convention to author a Vite/Rolldown/Rollup plugin as a factory function that returns the actual plugin object. The function can accept options which allows users to customize the behavior of the plugin.
:::

### Transforming Custom File Types

```js
const fileRegex = /\.(my-file-ext)$/ 

export default function myPlugin() {
  return {
    name: 'transform-file',

    transform: {
      filter: {
        id: fileRegex,
      },
      handler(src, id) {
        return {
          code: compileFileToJS(src),
          map: null, // provide source map if available
        }
      },
    },
  }
}
```

### Importing a Virtual File

Virtual modules allow you to pass build time information to the source files using normal ESM import syntax. See [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules) for the full convention.

```js
import { exactRegex } from '@rolldown/pluginutils'

export default function myPlugin() {
  const virtualModuleId = 'virtual:my-module'
  const resolvedVirtualModuleId = '\0' + virtualModuleId

  return {
    name: 'my-plugin', // required, will show up in warnings and errors
    resolveId: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(virtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return resolvedVirtualModuleId
      },
    },
    load: {
      filter: { id: exactRegex(resolvedVirtualModuleId) },
      handler() {
        return `export const msg = "from virtual module"`
      },
    },
  }
}
```

Which allows importing the module in JavaScript:

```js
import { msg } from 'virtual:my-module'

console.log(msg)
```

In Vite, since `\0` is not a permitted char in import URLs, a `\0{id}` virtual id ends up encoded as `/@id/__x00__{id}` during dev in the browser. The id is decoded back before entering the plugins pipeline, so this is not seen by plugin hooks code.
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Rolldown Hooks
  Topic: Hooks available during development and build
  Summary: Description of Rolldown hooks invoked by Vite dev server, categorized by when they are called (server start, module request, server close).
  Keywords: Rolldown Hooks, dev server, build hooks, per-environment hooks
  Entities: plugin container, options hook, buildStart hook, resolveId hook, load hook, transform hook, buildEnd hook, closeBundle hook, moduleParsed hook

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Rolldown Hooks

During dev, the Vite dev server creates a plugin container that invokes [Rolldown Build Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#build-hooks) the same way Rolldown does it.

All rolldown hooks are [per-environment hooks](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks).

The following hooks are called once on server start:

- [`options`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#options)
- [`buildStart`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildstart)

The following hooks are called on each incoming module request:

- [`resolveId`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#resolveid)
- [`load`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#load)
- [`transform`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#transform)

These hooks also have an extended `options` parameter with additional Vite-specific properties. You can read more in the [SSR documentation](/guide/ssr#ssr-specific-plugin-logic).

Some `resolveId` calls' `importer` value may be an absolute path for a generic `index.html` at root as it's not always possible to derive the actual importer due to Vite's unbundled dev server pattern. For imports handled within Vite's resolve pipeline, the importer can be tracked during the import analysis phase, providing the correct `importer` value.

The following hooks are called when the server is closed:

- [`buildEnd`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#buildend)
- [`closeBundle`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#closebundle)

Note that the [`moduleParsed`](https://rolldown.rs/reference/Interface.Plugin#moduleparsed) hook is **not** called during dev, because Vite avoids full AST parses for better performance.

[Output Generation Hooks](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#output-generation-hooks) (except `closeBundle`) are **not** called during dev.
````

# SECTION METADATA

````markdown
## Vite Specific Hooks

Vite plugins can also provide hooks that serve Vite-specific purposes. These hooks are ignored by Rollup.

### `config`

- **Type:** `(config: UserConfig, env: { mode: 'build' | 'serve', command: string, isSsrBuild?: boolean, isPreview?: boolean }) => UserConfig | null | void`
- **Kind:** `async`, `sequential`
- **Scope:** [Global](/guide/api-environment-plugins#per-environment-hooks-and-global-hooks)

  Modify Vite config before it's resolved. The hook recei
````

[2026-09-09T19:28:00.3490570Z] [OUTPUT:STAGE3_OUTPUT_segsegment-13] Length: 2546 chars (truncated to 40k for log)
{
  "chunks": [
    {

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API
  Topic: Introduction to Vite Plugin API
  Summary: Overview of Vite plugins extending Rolldown's interface, recommendation to read Rolldown docs first, and advice on checking existing patterns before authoring.

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
# Plugin API

Vite plugins extends Rolldown's plugin interface with a few extra Vite-specific options. As a result, you can write a Vite plugin once and have it work for both dev and build.

**It is recommended to go through [Rolldown's plugin documentation](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api) first before reading the sections below.**
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Authoring a Plugin
  Topic: Guidelines for creating new Vite plugins
  Summary: Advice on checking existing features and community plugins before creating a new one, and suggestions for inline plugin usage and sharing.
  Keywords: Authoring, vite.config.js, community plugins, sharing
  Entities: vite.config.js, vite-plugin-inspect

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Authoring a Plugin

Vite strives to offer established patterns out of the box, so before creating a new plugin make sure that you check the [Features guide](/guide/features) to see if your need is covered. Also review available community plugins, both in the form of a [compatible Rollup plugin](https://github.com/rollup/awesome) and [Vite Specific plugins](https://github.com/vitejs/awesome-vite#plugins).

When creating a plugin, you can inline it in your `vite.config.js`. There is no need to create a new package for it. Once you see that a plugin was useful in your projects, consider sharing it to help others [in the ecosystem](https://chat.vite.dev).

::: tip
When learning, debugging, or authoring plugins, we suggest including [vite-plugin-inspect](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect) in your project. It allows you to inspect the intermediate state of Vite plugins. After installing, you can visit `localhost:5173/__inspect/` to inspect the modules and transformation stack of your project. Check out install instructions in the [vite-plugin-inspect docs](https://github.com/antfu/vite-plugin-inspect).
![vite-plugin-inspect](../images/vite-plugin-inspect.webp)
:::
````

# SECTION METADATA
  Heading path: Plugin API → Conventions
  Topic: Naming and packaging conventions for plugins
  Summary: Guidelines for naming Rolldown and Vite plugins, including prefixes and keywords for package.json, and specific prefixes for framework plugins.
  Keywords: naming conventions, package.json, keywords, prefix
  Entities: package.json, keywords field

# SOURCE CONTENT
````markdown
## Conventions

If the plugin doesn't use Vite specific hooks and can be implemented as a [Compatible Rolldown Plugin](#rolldown-plugin-compatibility), then it is recommended to use the [Rolldown Plugin naming conventions](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#conventions).

- Rolldown Plugins should have a clear name with `rolldown-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `rolldown-plugin` and `vite-plugin` keywords in package.json `keywords` field.

This exposes the plugin to be also used in pure Rolldown or Rollup based projects.

For Vite only plugins

- Vite Plugins should have a clear name with `vite-plugin-` prefix.
- Include `vite-plugin` keyword in package.json `keywords` field.
- Include a section in the plugin docs detailing why it is a Vite only plugin (for example, it uses Vite specific plugin hooks).

If your plugin is only going to work for a particular framework, its name should be included as part of the prefix.

- `vite-plugin-vue-` prefix for Vue Plugins
- `vite-plugin-react-` prefix for React Plugins
- `vite-plugin-svelte-` prefix for Svelte Plugins

See also [Virtual Modules Convention](https://rolldown.rs/apis/plugin-api#virtual-modules).
````
